Hi, doctor. Thank you so much for seeing me. Um, I hope I'm not taking up too much of your time. My manager actually suggested I come in. I'm a waitress, and usually, I'm really good at my job, I promise. It's just... I've been a little off lately, I guess. 

I'm a little embarrassed to even bring it up, but... what scared everyone at work is that I started sort of freezing up in the middle of service. Or, you know, doing things out of order. Like, the other day I ended up just standing by the soda station holding the cash drawer key, and I was just repeating table numbers to myself instead of actually closing out the checks. 

It sounds so silly when I say it out loud, doesn't it? I think I just got a little overwhelmed and distracted, but I really want to get back to doing a good job. Is that... does that make sense for why I'd be here?

It's just... suddenly I'm standing there and I realize I'm not doing what I'm supposed to be doing. Or maybe I'm just getting too caught up in trying to make sure I do everything perfectly for the customers. Does that sound like a normal thing that happens to people sometimes?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or nervous?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I wouldn't say I'm an anxious person, generally. I mean, I try really hard to keep a positive attitude, especially for the customers. But that's just normal caring about your work, isn't it? I don't think I feel, like, overly anxious or anything that would be a problem. I really just want to be helpful and do things right. Is it okay to feel a little nervous about that sometimes?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're outside of your body, watching yourself do things?\n\nPatient: Um... oh, no, I don't think so. That sounds really scary, to be honest. \n\nI mean, sometimes I guess I might zone out just a tiny bit if I'm really tired after a long shift, but I'm always still... me. In my own body, you know? Um... well, I wouldn't say I'm not in control, exactly. I mean, I always want to do my job right, and I try my best to be a good employee.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... no, not really. I mean, I try not to avoid anything. I want to be a reliable team member, you know? 

I guess... well, maybe I get a little nervous if I have to handle a really big party all by myself, just in case I freeze up and keep them waiting. But I still do it! I don't say no if my manager asks me to take a table. I just try to push through it and focus really hard on getting everything right. 

I don't want to be the kind of person who makes excuses or leaves all the hard work for the other servers. I'm trying really hard to be a good employee. Is it okay that I still try to do everything? I hope that shows I'm really trying to get better.

Um... well, I guess maybe a little bit? I mean, I really try not to let it affect anything, but... I suppose it has been a bit harder lately. 

I've been trying so hard to get this shift-lead position at work, and I really thought I was going to get it. And, um, at home... my husband mentioned that I seem like I'm barely there with him anymore. I don't mean to ignore him, I just... I get so caught up in my head trying to keep everything straight. I'm really trying to fix it, though! I want to be a good wife and a good employee.

I really wish I could give you a good answer for that. I've tried so hard to figure out if there's a pattern, you know? It's just... suddenly I'm standing there and I realize I'm not doing what I'm supposed to be doing. Or maybe I'm just getting too caught up in trying to make sure I do everything perfectly for the customers. Does that sound like a normal thing that happens to people sometimes?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or nervous?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I wouldn't say I'm an anxious person, generally. I mean, I try really hard to keep a positive attitude, especially for the customers. Is it okay to feel a little nervous about that sometimes?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're outside of your body, watching yourself do things?\n\nPatient: Um... oh, no, I don't think so. I'm not, like, floating above myself or watching from the outside or anything strange like that. \n\nI hope it's okay that I say no to that one. I really want to make sure I'm giving you the information you need to figure this out. Is it alright that I haven't experienced that? I just want to make sure I'm answering everything the right way for you.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're in a daze or disconnected from what's happening around you?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't think so? I mean, I try really hard to stay focused, especially when I'm at the restaurant. It's so busy, you really can't afford to be in a daze, you know? \n\nI guess... sometimes if it's super loud and there are a lot of people talking at once, I might get a little overwhelmed just for a second, but I wouldn't call it being disconnected. I'm always trying to pay attention to what my tables need. I'm really sorry if that's not the kind of answer you're looking for. I just... I want to be honest with you, and I don't think I feel disconnected like that. Does that make sense? I'm always trying to pay attention to what my tables need. I'm really sorry if that's not the kind of answer you're looking for. I just... I want to be honest with you, and I don't think I feel disconnected like that. Does that make sense? I didn't mean to do it, I promise. I just... sometimes I get a little mixed up? Like, I started freezing up right in the middle of a busy service. Or I'd do things in the wrong order. \n\nLike, one time, instead of closing out checks, I just stood by the soda station holding the key to the cash drawer, and I kept repeating table numbers out loud. I don't know why I did it, and I felt so bad because I know it probably looked really weird to my manager and the customers. But I'm not trying to be difficult or anything, I swear. I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information so you can help me be better at my job again.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself unable to recall what you were doing or what happened just before one of these episodes of freezing up or doing things out of order?\n\nPatient: Um... Is it okay if I don't really know for sure?\n\nDoctor: Do you remember any specific events or thoughts that were going through your mind right before you froze up or did things out of order?\n\nPatient: Um... I really wish I could give you a good answer for that. I've tried so hard to figure out if there's a pattern, you know? Just to make sure I'm not doing something wrong. \n\nBut honestly, I don't really remember thinking anything specific right before it happens. It's just... suddenly I'm standing there and I realize I'm not doing what I'm supposed to be doing. I'm really sorry, I know you're trying to figure this out and I feel like I'm not being very helpful. I just... my mind kind of goes blank, I think? Or maybe I'm just getting too caught up in trying to make sure I do everything perfectly for the customers. Does that sound like a normal thing that happens to people sometimes?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or nervous?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I wouldn't say I'm an anxious person, generally. I mean, I try really hard to keep a positive attitude, especially for the customers. You kind of have to in my job, right? \n\nI guess... sometimes I get a little nervous about making sure I'm doing a good job, or hoping I don't let my manager down. But that's just normal caring about your work, isn't it? I don't think I feel, like, overly anxious or anything that would be a problem. I really just want to be helpful and do things right. Is it okay to feel a little nervous about that sometimes?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're outside of your body, watching yourself do things?\n\nPatient: Um... oh, no, I don't think so. That sounds really scary, to be honest. \n\nI mean, sometimes I guess I might zone out just a tiny bit if I'm really tired after a long shift, but I'm always still... me. In my own body, you know? I'm not, like, floating above myself or watching from the outside or anything strange like that. \n\nI hope it's okay that I say no to that one. I really want to make sure I'm giving you the information you need to figure this out. Is it alright that I haven't experienced that? I just want to make sure I'm answering everything the right way for you.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're in a daze or disconnected from what's happening around you?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't think so? I mean, I try really hard to stay focused, especially when I'm at the restaurant. It's so busy, you really can't afford to be in a daze, you know? \n\nI guess... sometimes if it's super loud and there are a lot of people talking at once, I might get a little overwhelmed just for a second, but I wouldn't call it being disconnected. I'm always trying to pay attention to what my tables need. I'm really sorry if that's not the kind of answer you're looking for. I just... I want to be honest with you, and I don't think I feel disconnected like that. Does that make sense? I hope I'm explaining it okay.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're not in control of your actions?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I wouldn't say I'm not in control, exactly. I mean, I always want to do my job right, and I try my best to be a good employee. \n\nBut I guess... well, there was this one thing that happened at work, and it kind of scared everyone. I didn't mean to do it, I promise. I just... sometimes I get a little mixed up? Like, I started freezing up right in the middle of a busy service. Or I'd do things in the wrong order. \n\nLike, one time, instead of closing out checks, I just stood by the soda station holding the key to the cash drawer, and I kept repeating table numbers out loud. I don't know why I did it, and I felt so bad because I know it probably looked really weird to my manager and the customers. But I'm not trying to be difficult or anything, I swear. Is it okay that I'm telling you this? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information so you can help me be better at my job again.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself unable to recall what you were doing or what happened just before one of these episodes of freezing up or doing things out of order?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't think so? I mean, I usually remember everything leading up to it. Like, I'll know I was just talking to table four about their drink order, or I was just walking back from the kitchen. \n\nI don't really lose time or forget what I was doing right before. It's just... the freezing up part that gets a little confusing. But I definitely remember everything else. I hope that's a good thing? Like, it means it's not too bad, right? I just really want to make sure I'm giving you the right details. Is it helpful that I can remember what happened right before?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're not really yourself or like something is taking over your actions?\n\nPatient: Um... no, I don't think so. I mean, I'm always me. I don't feel like someone else or... or like something is taking over. \n\nI know I said I get mixed up sometimes and do things I didn't mean to, like standing by the soda station, but it's still me doing it. It's not like I'm possessed or anything scary like that. I just... I get a little stuck sometimes, I guess? But I'm always trying my best to be normal and do a good job. \n\nI'm really sorry if my answers are confusing. I just want to make sure you know I'm not, like, completely out of control. I'm still myself. Is that... does that make sense? I hope I'm answering this the right way for you.\n\nDoctor: Do you tend to avoid certain situations or tasks at work because you're afraid of having one of these episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I mean, I try not to avoid anything. I want to be a reliable team member, you know? \n\nI guess... well, maybe I get a little nervous if I have to handle a really big party all by myself, just in case I freeze up and keep them waiting. But I still do it! I don't say no if my manager asks me to take a table. I just try to push through it and focus really hard on getting everything right. \n\nI don't want to be the kind of person who makes excuses or leaves all the hard work for the other servers. I'm trying really hard to be a good employee. Is it okay that I still try to do everything? I hope that shows I'm really trying to get better.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever turn down tasks or responsibilities at work because of concerns about freezing up or doing things out of order?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I don't think I do that. I mean, I think you just asked me something like this, but I really don't turn things down. \n\nI don't want my manager to think I'm slacking off or that I can't handle my job. So if they need me to roll silverware or restock the ice or take a big section, I just say yes. I really try to be helpful, even if I'm a little worried I might get stuck for a second. I just... I want to be someone they can count on. \n\nIs it good that I keep trying to do everything? I really want to be doing the right thing here.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your episodes of freezing up or doing things out of order are affecting your daily life or overall well-being?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I guess maybe a little bit? I mean, I really try not to let it affect anything, but... I suppose it has been a bit harder lately. \n\nI've been trying so hard to get this shift-lead position at work, and I really thought I was going to get it. But my manager pulled me aside and said my mistakes and... and getting confused during service and closing are kind of causing problems. So I might not get it now, which is really disappointing because I want to be doing a good job for them. \n\nAnd, um, at home... my husband mentioned that I seem like I'm barely there with him anymore. I don't mean to ignore him, I just... I get so caught up in my head trying to keep everything straight. I'm really trying to fix it, though! I want to be a good wife and a good employee. \n\nIs it normal to struggle a little bit with balancing everything when you're stressed? I hope I'm not sounding like I'm just giving up, because I'm definitely still trying my best.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your episodes of freezing up or doing things out of order are causing you a lot of distress?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I mean, I wouldn't say it's a *lot* of distress. But I don't let it get me down too much. I just take a deep breath and tell myself to try harder next time. I think everyone gets a little flustered sometimes, right? \n\nI really don't want to sound like a complainer. I'm honestly doing okay, and I'm just focusing on being better. That's the right way to handle it, isn't it? Just staying positive?\n\nDoctor: Do you avoid talking to your manager about your performance because of your episodes of freezing up or doing things out of order?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I wouldn't say I avoid talking to my manager. I mean, I actually try to check in with him when I can, just to make sure I'm doing what they need me to do. \n\nI guess I don't necessarily go out of my way to bring up the times I freeze up, but that's just because I don't want to make a big deal out of it and bother him. He's so busy, you know? I want to show him I can handle things on my own. I just try to listen to his feedback and do better next time. \n\nThat's the best way to handle it, right? Just focusing on improving instead of dwelling on the mistakes? I really want him to see me as a good employee.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever avoid certain tasks or situations outside of work because you're worried you might freeze up or do things out of order?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I don't think I avoid anything. I mean, I try to keep up with all my normal stuff—grocery shopping, running errands, seeing friends when I can. \n\nI guess sometimes if I'm feeling really overwhelmed or tired, I might ask my husband to run to the store for me instead, but that's just normal tired, you know? Not because I'm worried about freezing up. I really try to push through and just do what needs to be done. I want to make sure the house is taken care of and everything is normal. \n\nI think it's pretty normal to want a break sometimes, right? I just try to be as helpful as I can be most of the time.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I really try my best to push through it so I don't inconvenience my coworkers or the customers, I promise. I'm sorry, I know that's probably not a very helpful answer. Is it okay if I don't really know for sure?\n\nDoctor: Do you remember any specific events or thoughts that were going through your mind right before you froze up or did things out of order?\n\nPatient: Um... I really wish I could give you a good answer for that. I've tried so hard to figure out if there's a pattern, you know? Just to make sure I'm not doing something wrong. \n\nBut honestly, I don't really remember thinking anything specific right before it happens. It's just... suddenly I'm standing there and I realize I'm not doing what I'm supposed to be doing. I'm really sorry, I know you're trying to figure this out and I feel like I'm not being very helpful. I just... my mind kind of goes blank, I think? Or maybe I'm just getting too caught up in trying to make sure I do everything perfectly for the customers. Does that sound like a normal thing that happens to people sometimes?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or nervous?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I wouldn't say I'm an anxious person, generally. I mean, I try really hard to keep a positive attitude, especially for the customers. You kind of have to in my job, right? \n\nI guess... sometimes I get a little nervous about making sure I'm doing a good job, or hoping I don't let my manager down. But that's just normal caring about your work, isn't it? I don't think I feel, like, overly anxious or anything that would be a problem. I really just want to be helpful and do things right. Is it okay to feel a little nervous about that sometimes?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're outside of your body, watching yourself do things?\n\nPatient: Um... oh, no, I don't think so. That sounds really scary, to be honest. \n\nI mean, sometimes I guess I might zone out just a tiny bit if I'm really tired after a long shift, but I'm always still... me. In my own body, you know? I'm not, like, floating above myself or watching from the outside or anything strange like that. \n\nI hope it's okay that I say no to that one. I really want to make sure I'm giving you the information you need to figure this out. Is it alright that I haven't experienced that? I just want to make sure I'm answering everything the right way for you.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're in a daze or disconnected from what's happening around you?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't think so? I mean, I try really hard to stay focused, especially when I'm at the restaurant. It's so busy, you really can't afford to be in a daze, you know? \n\nI guess... sometimes if it's super loud and there are a lot of people talking at once, I might get a little overwhelmed just for a second, but I wouldn't call it being disconnected. I'm always trying to pay attention to what my tables need. I'm really sorry if that's not the kind of answer you're looking for. I just... I want to be honest with you, and I don't think I feel disconnected like that. Does that make sense? I hope I'm explaining it okay.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're not in control of your actions?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I wouldn't say I'm not in control, exactly. I mean, I always want to do my job right, and I try my best to be a good employee. \n\nBut I guess... well, there was this one thing that happened at work, and it kind of scared everyone. I didn't mean to do it, I promise. I just... sometimes I get a little mixed up? Like, I started freezing up right in the middle of a busy service. Or I'd do things in the wrong order. \n\nLike, one time, instead of closing out checks, I just stood by the soda station holding the key to the cash drawer, and I kept repeating table numbers out loud. I don't know why I did it, and I felt so bad because I know it probably looked really weird to my manager and the customers. But I'm not trying to be difficult or anything, I swear. Is it okay that I'm telling you this? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information so you can help me be better at my job again.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself unable to recall what you were doing or what happened just before one of these episodes of freezing up or doing things out of order?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't think so? I mean, I usually remember everything leading up to it. Like, I'll know I was just talking to table four about their drink order, or I was just walking back from the kitchen. \n\nI don't really lose time or forget what I was doing right before. It's just... the freezing up part that gets a little confusing. But I definitely remember everything else. I hope that's a good thing? Like, it means it's not too bad, right? I just really want to make sure I'm giving you the right details. Is it helpful that I can remember what happened right before?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're not really yourself or like something is taking over your actions?\n\nPatient: Um... no, I don't think so. I mean, I'm always me. I don't feel like someone else or... or like something is taking over. \n\nI know I said I get mixed up sometimes and do things I didn't mean to, like standing by the soda station, but it's still me doing it. It's not like I'm possessed or anything scary like that. I just... I get a little stuck sometimes, I guess? But I'm always trying my best to be normal and do a good job. \n\nI'm really sorry if my answers are confusing. I just want to make sure you know I'm not, like, completely out of control. I'm still myself. Is that... does that make sense? I hope I'm answering this the right way for you.\n\nDoctor: Do you tend to avoid certain situations or tasks at work because you're afraid of having one of these episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I mean, I try not to avoid anything. I want to be a reliable team member, you know? \n\nI guess... well, maybe I get a little nervous if I have to handle a really big party all by myself, just in case I freeze up and keep them waiting. But I still do it! I don't say no if my manager asks me to take a table. I just try to push through it and focus really hard on getting everything right. \n\nI don't want to be the kind of person who makes excuses or leaves all the hard work for the other servers. I'm trying really hard to be a good employee. Is it okay that I still try to do everything? I hope that shows I'm really trying to get better.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever turn down tasks or responsibilities at work because of concerns about freezing up or doing things out of order?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I don't think I do that. I mean, I think you just asked me something like this, but I really don't turn things down. \n\nI don't want my manager to think I'm slacking off or that I can't handle my job. So if they need me to roll silverware or restock the ice or take a big section, I just say yes. I really try to be helpful, even if I'm a little worried I might get stuck for a second. I just... I want to be someone they can count on. \n\nIs it good that I keep trying to do everything? I really want to be doing the right thing here.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your episodes of freezing up or doing things out of order are affecting your daily life or overall well-being?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I guess maybe a little bit? I mean, I really try not to let it affect anything, but... I suppose it has been a bit harder lately. \n\nI've been trying so hard to get this shift-lead position at work, and I really thought I was going to get it. But my manager pulled me aside and said my mistakes and... and getting confused during service and closing are kind of causing problems. So I might not get it now, which is really disappointing because I want to be doing a good job for them. \n\nAnd, um, at home... my husband mentioned that I seem like I'm barely there with him anymore. I don't mean to ignore him, I just... I get so caught up in my head trying to keep everything straight. I'm really trying to fix it, though! I want to be a good wife and a good employee. \n\nIs it normal to struggle a little bit with balancing everything when you're stressed? I hope I'm not sounding like I'm just giving up, because I'm definitely still trying my best.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your episodes of freezing up or doing things out of order are causing you a lot of distress?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I mean, I wouldn't say it's a *lot* of distress. I try to stay really positive about things! \n\nI guess I get a little frustrated with myself sometimes when I mess up, just because I want everything to go smoothly, you know? But I don't let it get me down too much. I just take a deep breath and tell myself to try harder next time. I think everyone gets a little flustered sometimes, right? \n\nI really don't want to sound like a complainer. I'm honestly doing okay, and I'm just focusing on being better. That's the right way to handle it, isn't it? Just staying positive?\n\nDoctor: Do you avoid talking to your manager about your performance because of your episodes of freezing up or doing things out of order?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I wouldn't say I avoid talking to my manager. I mean, I actually try to check in with him when I can, just to make sure I'm doing what they need me to do. \n\nI guess I don't necessarily go out of my way to bring up the times I freeze up, but that's just because I don't want to make a big deal out of it and bother him. He's so busy, you know? I want to show him I can handle things on my own. I just try to listen to his feedback and do better next time. \n\nThat's the best way to handle it, right? Just focusing on improving instead of dwelling on the mistakes? I really want him to see me as a good employee.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever avoid certain tasks or situations outside of work because you're worried you might freeze up or do things out of order?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. Not because I'm worried about freezing up. I really try to push through and just do what needs to be done. I want to make sure the house is taken care of and everything is normal. \n\nI think it's pretty normal to want a break sometimes, right? I just try to be as helpful as I can be most of the time.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're observing yourself from outside your body or like you're in a dream?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no. No, I don't think I've ever felt anything like that. It's definitely not like I'm outside my body or in a dream or anything like that. I always stay right here in the moment. \n\nI try to be really grounded and present, especially when I'm taking care of customers. That's a good thing, right? To just stay focused on where you are?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you are disconnected from yourself or your surroundings?\n\nPatient: Um, no... no, I wouldn't say I feel disconnected. It's better to stay sharp and aware like that, isn't it?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding social situations because of your episodes of freezing up or doing things out of order?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not at all.     "traumatic_stressor": null,
```json
    "psychosocial_stressor": true,
    "additional_requirements": ["symptoms are causing significant impairment in social and occupational functioning"]
  }
}
```
